<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove that a globally Weil-ℚ-factorial compact Kähler klt fourfold pair with effective rational boundary and canonical rational line bundle admits a finite ordinary minimal model program starting on the given pair. It ends at a nef model when the adjoint class is pseudo-effective and at a Mori fibre space otherwise.

- **claim-finite-ordinary-program** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-finite)): Let $X$ be a normal globally Weil $\mathbb Q$-factorial compact Kähler fourfold, with a canonical Weil divisor $K_X$, and let $\Delta\geq0$ be a rational divisor such that $(X,\Delta)$ is klt. Then there is a finite sequence of ordinary negative steps $$(X,\Delta)=(X_0,\Delta_0)\dashrightarrow\cdots \dashrightarrow(X_n,\Delta_n).$$ Every $X_i$ is normal, compact Kähler and globally Weil $\mathbb Q$-factorial; every $(X_i,\Delta_i)$ is klt, and $D_i=K_{X_i}+\Delta_i$ is $\mathbb Q$-Cartier. The canonical divisors are the transforms of the specified datum. The following alternatives hold.* 1. *If $D=K_X+\Delta$ is pseudo-effective, then $D_n$ is nef. The composite $\phi:X\dashrightarrow X_n$ extracts no prime divisor, and $$a(E;X,\Delta)\leq a(E;X_n,\Delta_n)$$ for every prime divisor over the two models, with strict inequality for every prime on $X$ contracted by $\phi$.* 2. *If $D$ is not pseudo-effective, there is a projective surjective morphism $g:X_n\to S$ with connected fibres, where $S$ is normal compact Kähler, $\dim S<4$, $\rho(X_n/S)=1$, and $-D_n$ is $g$-ample.* *For each negative contraction, including $g$, the relative Bott–Chern dimension is one. If $X$ is globally strongly $\mathbb Q$-factorial, so is every $X_i$.* *The same conclusions hold in the intrinsic formulation with the canonical rational line bundle in place of a chosen canonical Weil divisor. In either formulation the first model is exactly $X$.
  - Explanation: For a globally Weil factorial compact Kähler fourfold with a klt rational pair, a finite sequence of ordinary negative steps exists. It starts on the given pair and keeps the category and the singularity class. It ends at a nef model when the adjoint is pseudo-effective and at a Mori fibre space over a lower-dimensional base otherwise.
- **claim-pullback-descent** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-pullback-image)): Let $f:T\to Z$ be a proper surjective morphism with connected fibres between normal compact analytic spaces with rational singularities. Suppose that $T$ is in class $\mathcal C$ and that either the general fibre is rationally connected or $R^jf_*\mathcal O_T=0$ for every $j>0$. Then $$f^*: H^{1,1}_{\mathrm{BC}}(Z)\longrightarrow H^{1,1}_{\mathrm{BC}}(T)$$ is injective, and its image consists exactly of the classes having degree zero on every $f$-vertical curve. If $T$ and $Z$ are Kähler, a class on $Z$ is nef if and only if its pullback is nef.
  - Explanation: Pullback of Bott-Chern classes along a fibre-connected proper surjection is injective. Its image consists of the classes of degree zero on vertical curves, and nefness is detected upstairs.
- **claim-exceptional-descent** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-positive-descent)): Let $p:U\to T$ be a resolution of a normal compact Kähler space, with $U$ compact Kähler. Let $\alpha\in H^{1,1}_{\mathrm{BC}}(T)$ and let $E$ be a $p$-exceptional real divisor. If $p^*\alpha+[E]$ is pseudo-effective, then $\alpha$ is pseudo-effective. No sign condition on $E$ is needed. Pseudo-effective classes pull back to resolutions, and nef classes on $T$ are pseudo-effective.
  - Explanation: Pseudo-effectivity descends along a resolution even when the exceptional correction has either sign, by extending plurisubharmonic potentials over codimension-two sets and normal singularities.
- **claim-negativity-strict-comparison** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-negativity)): 1. *Let $f:T\to Z$ be a proper bimeromorphic morphism of normal complex spaces and let $E$ be a real Cartier divisor such that $-E$ is $f$-nef. Then $E\geq0$ if and only if $f_*E\geq0$. Consequently an $f$-exceptional, $f$-numerically trivial real Cartier divisor is zero.* 2. *If, in addition, $f$ has connected projective fibres and $E\geq0$ is $f$-anti-nef, then every fibre is either contained in $\mathop{\mathrm{Supp}}E$ or disjoint from $\mathop{\mathrm{Supp}}E$.* 3. *Consider a nontrivial small birational diagram $$T\xrightarrow{\ f\ }Z\xleftarrow{\ f^+\ }T^+$$ of normal compact spaces, with projective contractions on both sides. Let $B\geq0$ be a real boundary, let $B^+$ be its strict transform, and suppose the ordinary adjoints $D=K_T+B$ and $D^+=K_{T^+}+B^+$ are real Cartier, with $-D$ relatively ample on the left and $D^+$ relatively ample on the right. The two exceptional loci have the same image $A\subset Z$. On a common resolution $p:V\to T$, $q:V\to T^+$, $$\label{eq:ordinary-flip-comparison} p^*D-q^*D^+=F,\qquad F\geq0,$$ where $F$ is an actual divisor exceptional over both models. For every prime divisor $P$ over these spaces, $$a(P,T^+,B^+)\geq a(P,T,B),$$ and the inequality is strict if the centre of $P$ on either side is contained in that side’s exceptional locus.* 4. *For the diagram in <span class="upright">(iii)</span>, if $\dim T=4$, then $\dim A\leq1$ and $$\dim\mathop{\mathrm{Exc}}(f)+\dim\mathop{\mathrm{Exc}}(f^+)\geq3.$$* 5. *The discrepancy conclusions in <span class="upright">(iii)</span> also hold for generalized pairs with the same fixed b-nef datum and transformed boundaries in a projective small diagram, provided the source log class has negative degree on every curve contracted on that side, and the target log class has positive degree on every curve contracted on the other side. They hold likewise for a projective divisorial contraction $f:T\to T'$ when the target boundary is the pushforward, the b-datum is unchanged, and the source log class has negative degree on every $f$-vertical curve. In this divisorial case strictness holds at every prime whose source centre lies in $\mathop{\mathrm{Exc}}(f)$. This part also applies when the fixed b-datum is nef only over a specified base.
  - Explanation: Negativity for actual divisors, a fibre dichotomy for anti-nef supports, and strict discrepancy comparison across small flips. It also gives dimension bounds on exceptional loci of fourfold flips, and carries over to generalized pairs with a fixed nef datum.
- **claim-exceptional-trace-relation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-class-trace)): Let $T$ be a normal compact Kähler space with rational singularities, marked birationally to $X$. Suppose that on a common smooth Kähler resolution $p:V\to T$, $q:V\to X$ there are a class $H_T\in H^{1,1}_{\mathrm{BC}}(T)$ and an actual $p$-exceptional real divisor $J_T$ satisfying $$\label{eq:class-trace} p^*H_T=q^*H+[J_T].$$ Then $J_T\geq0$. The class $H_T$ and the divisor $J_T$ are unique, and the correction on a higher resolution is the pullback of $J_T$.* *For an effective boundary $B_T$ with real Cartier ordinary adjoint, the generalized structure boundary for the nef datum $t\mathbf H$ is $$\label{eq:trace-boundary} B_{T,V}(t)=p^*(K_T+B_T)-K_V+tJ_T,\qquad t\geq0.$$ In particular, decreasing $t$ increases every generalized log discrepancy. If the generalized pair is gklt, its ordinary pair is klt; if all its exceptional log discrepancies exceed one, the ordinary pair is terminal.* *Conversely, suppose $t>0$ and a generalized pair with boundary $B_T$ and this fixed datum $t\mathbf H$ is given by <a href="#eq:generalized-structure" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:generalized-structure">[eq:generalized-structure]</a>, with log class $L$ and real Cartier ordinary adjoint. Then <a href="#eq:class-trace" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:class-trace">[eq:class-trace]</a> holds with $$H_T=\frac{L-[K_T+B_T]}{t}.$$
  - Explanation: The trace of the fixed Kähler form on a marked model splits uniquely as a class plus an effective exceptional divisor. This yields the structure boundary for the scaled nef datum and makes discrepancies grow as the scaling parameter decreases.
- **claim-trace-positivity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-big-trace)): Under the hypotheses of Lemma <a href="#lem:class-trace" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:class-trace">6</a>, the actual trace $\mathbf H_T$ has local plurisubharmonic potentials and represents $H_T$. The class $H_T$ is big.
  - Explanation: The actual trace current has local plurisubharmonic potentials, represents the trace class, and that class is big.
- **claim-small-model-comparison** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-small-comparison)): Let $(T,B_T+\mathbf M)$ and $(T',B_{T'}+\mathbf M)$ be generalized pairs with the same b-nef datum, whose log classes are $L_T$ and $L_{T'}$. Suppose the marked birational map $T\dashrightarrow T'$ extracts no divisors and $B_{T'}$ is the pushforward of $B_T$. On a common sufficiently high resolution $p:V\to T$, $q:V\to T'$, put $$F=B_{T,V}-B_{T',V}.$$ Then $F$ is an actual divisor exceptional over $T'$, and $$\label{eq:small-comparison} p^*L_T-q^*L_{T'}=[F],\qquad \operatorname{coeff}_P F =a(P,T',B_{T'}+\mathbf M)-a(P,T,B_T+\mathbf M).$$ If the map is small, $F$ is exceptional over both models. In this case nefness of $L_{T'}$ implies $F\geq0$, and nefness of both log classes implies $F=0$. These conclusions also hold relatively over a common base. Even without smallness, equality of the pullback classes in <a href="#eq:small-comparison" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:small-comparison">[eq:small-comparison]</a> implies equality of the structure boundaries.
  - Explanation: For two generalized pairs sharing a b-nef datum, the difference of log classes pulled back to a common resolution is an actual divisor whose coefficients are discrepancy differences. For small maps nefness forces it to be effective or zero.
- **claim-integral-descent** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-descent)): Let $f:T\to Z$ be a projective bimeromorphic contraction of normal complex spaces, and let $L$ be a line bundle numerically trivial over $Z$. Suppose that every point of $Z$ has a neighbourhood $U$ on which there is an ordinary rational klt pair $(f^{-1}U,C_U)$ with rational adjoint and $$L-(K_{f^{-1}U}+C_U)\quad\text{$f$-nef over $U$}.$$ Then $f_*L$ is a line bundle and the evaluation map is an isomorphism $$\label{eq:integral-descent} f^*f_*L\simeq L.$$ In particular the conclusion applies if the local ordinary adjoints are relatively antiample. It also applies to a global ordinary rational klt pair $(T,C)$ whenever $L-(K_T+C)$ is $f$-nef. Clearing one denominator therefore descends a rational line bundle as an actual rational line bundle.
  - Explanation: A line bundle numerically trivial over a projective bimeromorphic contraction, with a locally nef difference from a rational klt adjoint, descends as an actual line bundle with no extra tensor power.
- **claim-supports-kahler-margin** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-absolute-support)): Let $(T,B)$ be an ordinary rational klt pair on a normal compact Kähler space. Assume that $B$ is $\mathbb Q$-Cartier and that $D=K_T+B$ is a rational line bundle. If $D$ is not nef, $\overline{\mathrm{NA}}(T)$ has a $D$-negative extremal ray. Every such ray $R$ is generated by a rational curve and has a nonempty relatively open set $\mathcal U_R\subset R^\perp$ of classes satisfying $$\label{eq:absolute-support} \alpha\text{ is nef},\qquad \overline{\mathrm{NA}}(T)\cap\alpha^\perp=R,\qquad \alpha-c_1(D)\text{ is K\"ahler}.$$
  - Explanation: If the adjoint is not nef there is a negative extremal ray generated by a rational curve. Such a ray has a nonempty open set of nef supporting classes that exceed the adjoint by a Kähler class.
- **claim-relative-positivity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-relative-positivity)): Let $f:T\to Z$ be a proper morphism of compact complex spaces, with $T$ and $Z$ Kähler. Suppose that $L$ is a rational line bundle and $$c_1(L)=\kappa-f^*\gamma,$$ where $\kappa$ is Kähler on $T$ and $\gamma$ is a smooth real Bott–Chern class on $Z$. Then $L$ is $f$-ample and $f$ is projective. Conversely, a normal space projective over a compact Kähler space is compact Kähler if it has a global relatively ample line bundle.
  - Explanation: A class of the form Kähler minus a pullback from the base makes the line bundle relatively ample and the map projective. Conversely a relatively ample bundle over a Kähler base gives a Kähler total space.
- **claim-ordinary-negative-steps** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-ordinary-step)): Let $(T,B)$ be an ordinary rational klt pair on a normal compact Kähler space, globally $\mathbb Q$-factorial in the Weil-divisor sense, with canonical sheaf a rational line bundle. Put $D=K_T+B$. Every $D$-negative extremal ray $R\subset\overline{\mathrm{NA}}(T)$ has a projective contraction $f:T\to Z$ with connected fibres and normal compact Kähler target, such that $$\label{eq:ordinary-negative-bc} f^*H^{1,1}_{\mathrm{BC}}(Z)=R^\perp,\qquad \rho(T/Z)=1,\qquad -D\text{ is }f\text{-ample}.$$ Here $\rho$ is the rank of global line-bundle degrees on contracted curves. The target has rational singularities and $R^j f_*\mathcal O_T=0$ for $j>0$. If $\dim Z<\dim T$, this is a Mori fibre space. Otherwise $f$ is bimeromorphic, and precisely one of the following occurs.* 1. *The exceptional locus is one prime divisor $E$. Put $T'=Z$, $B'=f_*B$ and $D'=K_{T'}+B'$. Then $$\label{eq:ordinary-divisorial-difference} D=f^*D'+eE\qquad(e>0).$$* 2. *The contraction is small. For an adjoint Cartier index $r>0$, the intrinsic algebra $$\label{eq:ordinary-flip-algebra} \mathcal R_r=\bigoplus_{m\geq0}f_*\mathcal O_T(mrD)$$ is locally finitely generated, and $T'=\mathop{\mathrm{Proj}}_Z\mathcal R_r$ is the ordinary flip. It has a small projective morphism $f^+:T'\to Z$ with connected fibres. Put $B'=\phi_*B$ and $D'=K_{T'}+B'$, where $\phi:T\dashrightarrow T'$ is the induced small map. For a sufficiently divisible choice of $r$ there is an actual isomorphism $$\label{eq:ordinary-tautological} \mathcal O_{\mathop{\mathrm{Proj}}_Z\mathcal R_r}(1)\simeq\mathcal O_{T'}(rD').$$ In particular $D'$ is a rational line bundle and is $f^+$-ample. Passing to a sufficiently divisible Veronese leaves $T'$ unchanged.* *In both birational cases, $T'$ is compact Kähler and globally Weil-divisor $\mathbb Q$-factorial, its canonical sheaf is a rational line bundle, and $(T',B')$ is klt. Strong global $\mathbb Q$-factoriality is preserved when imposed on $T$. A supplied global canonical Weil divisor is transported to a canonical Weil divisor on $T'$. No prime divisor is extracted. All log discrepancies weakly increase; the increase is strict for every original prime contracted by the step, and, for a flip, for every place centred in either exceptional locus.
  - Explanation: Every negative extremal ray has a projective contraction with the stated Bott-Chern and rank properties. It is a Mori fibre space, a divisorial contraction, or a small contraction with an ordinary flip. The category, factoriality and klt property are preserved, and discrepancies weakly increase.
- **claim-class-transport** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-class-transport)): For a birational step of Proposition <a href="#prop:ordinary-step" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:ordinary-step">12</a>, let $f':T'\to Z$ mean $f^+$ in the small case and $\mathrm{id}_Z$ in the divisorial case. Every $\theta\in H^{1,1}_{\mathrm{BC}}(T)$ has a unique expression $\theta=f^*\eta+s\,c_1(D)$, with $\eta\in H^{1,1}_{\mathrm{BC}}(Z)$ and $s\in\mathbb R$. Define $$\label{eq:class-transport} \theta'= (f')^*\eta+s\,c_1(D').$$ On a common resolution, $p^*\theta-q^*\theta'$ is the class of an actual divisor exceptional over $T'$. This rule sends the class of a global Weil divisor to that of its transform or pushforward. Moreover, it propagates the fixed-nef-part trace relation of Lemma <a href="#lem:class-trace" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:class-trace">6</a>, with an actual effective exceptional correction on the new model. The same conclusions hold for ordinary real-boundary flips on strongly globally $\mathbb Q$-factorial compact Kähler spaces constructed by (Hacon and Xie 2026, Proposition 4.2) [[HX2026](#ref-HX2026)].
  - Explanation: Bott-Chern classes decompose uniquely over the contraction base plus a multiple of the adjoint and are transported across a step. The transport differs by an actual exceptional divisor and propagates the fixed-trace relation with an effective correction.
- **claim-cycle-loss** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-cycle-loss)): Let $X\dashrightarrow Y$ be a bimeromorphic transformation of compact Kähler spaces, represented by a proper bimeromorphic diagram. Suppose it identifies $$U=X\setminus A\simeq Y\setminus B,$$ where $A$ and $B$ are closed analytic subspaces and $\dim B<k$. Then there is a surjection $$\mathcal C_k(X)\longrightarrow\mathcal C_k(Y).$$ If $A$ contains an irreducible compact analytic $k$-dimensional subspace, then $c_k(X)>c_k(Y)$.* *Consequently, in a sequence of bimeromorphic transformations of $n$-dimensional compact Kähler spaces which extract no prime divisors, only finitely many transformations contract a prime divisor. A small transformation preserves $c_{n-1}$.
  - Explanation: Cycle spans surject under bimeromorphic maps that are isomorphisms off small sets, and drop strictly when a compact subspace of the right dimension is contracted. Hence only finitely many steps contract a prime divisor, while small steps preserve the codimension-one count.
- **claim-low-places-finite** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-low-places)): Let $(X,B+\mathbf M)$ be a generalized pair on a compact normal complex space, with effective real boundary of finite support and nef b-$(1,1)$ data descending to a model projective over $X$. Fix a projective log resolution $p:W\to X$ carrying those data.* 1. *If the pair is generalized klt, there is $\epsilon>0$ such that every prime divisor over $X$ has log discrepancy at least $\epsilon$, and only finitely many exceptional places have $$a(E;X,B+\mathbf M)<1+\epsilon.$$* 2. *If the pair is generalized terminal and $b=\max(\{0\}\cup\{\operatorname{coeff}_D B\})$, there are only finitely many exceptional places with $$a(E;X,B+\mathbf M)<2-b.$$* *In each assertion the finite set of exceptional places can be represented simultaneously by prime divisors on a smooth model projective over $X$. In particular, any prescribed finite set of exceptional places with $a(E;X,B+\mathbf M)\leq 1$ for a generalized klt pair has such a realization. This conclusion does not use an extraction or termination theorem.
  - Explanation: For generalized klt pairs, and for terminal ones with the sharper cutoff, only finitely many exceptional places have low log discrepancy. These can be realized together by prime divisors on one smooth projective model.
- **claim-terminal-smooth-codim-two** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-terminal-codim-two)): Let $(T,\Phi)$ be an ordinary klt pair on a normal complex fourfold, with effective real boundary, $K_T$ a $\mathbb Q$-line bundle, and $a(E;T,\Phi)>1$ for every exceptional prime divisor $E$ over $T$. Then $T$ is smooth in codimension two.
  - Explanation: A klt pair on a fourfold whose exceptional log discrepancies exceed one forces the ambient space to be smooth in codimension two, by slicing with a surface.
- **claim-terminal-low-centres** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-terminal-low-centres)): Let $(T,\Phi)$ be an ordinary klt pair on a compact normal complex fourfold, where $\Phi\geq0$ is real and $a(E;T,\Phi)>1$ for every exceptional prime divisor $E$ over $T$. Apart from finitely many exceptional places, every exceptional place with $a(E;T,\Phi)<2$ has centre a surface $S\subset T$ with the following properties: $T$ and the reduced support of $\Phi$ are generically smooth along $S$, exactly one positive boundary component contains $S$, and, if its coefficient is $b$, then $$a(E;T,\Phi)\geq 2-b.$$ All places above such a surface are allowed in this conclusion, not just the exceptional divisor of its first blowup.
  - Explanation: Apart from finitely many exceptional places, those of log discrepancy below the cutoff sit over a surface in exactly one boundary component and obey a lower bound in the coefficient. All valuations above such a surface are covered.
- **claim-terminal-real-flips-terminate** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-terminal-real-four)): Let $(T_0,\Phi_0)$ be an ordinary klt pair on a normal globally $\mathbb Q$-factorial compact Kähler fourfold, with $K_{T_0}$ a $\mathbb Q$-line bundle and effective real boundary. Suppose $a(E;T_0,\Phi_0)>1$ for every exceptional prime divisor $E$ over $T_0$. Every sequence of ordinary $(K_{T_i}+\Phi_i)$-flips starting at this pair terminates, provided all models are normal globally $\mathbb Q$-factorial compact Kähler fourfolds, each $K_{T_i}$ is a $\mathbb Q$-line bundle, and each flip is given by projective small contractions $$T_i\xrightarrow{\ f_i\ }Z_i \xleftarrow{\ f_i^+\ }T_{i+1}$$ with $-(K_{T_i}+\Phi_i)$ relatively ample for $f_i$ and $K_{T_{i+1}}+\Phi_{i+1}$ relatively ample for $f_i^+$. Here $\Phi_{i+1}$ is the strict transform of $\Phi_i$. No pseudo-effectivity or bigness assumption is required.
  - Explanation: Ordinary flip sequences on terminal real-boundary fourfold pairs terminate. Flipped surfaces are eliminated by a finite witness count, flipping surfaces in the boundary by a surface-class rank, and the remaining flips by the analytic cycle count.
- **claim-terminal-empty-boundary-termination** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-terminal-four)): Let $X_0$ be a normal globally strongly $\mathbb Q$-factorial compact Kähler fourfold with terminal singularities. Every sequence starting at $X_0$ of ordinary empty-boundary negative divisorial contractions and flips, with the projectivity and compact Kähler conclusions of Proposition <a href="#prop:ordinary-step" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:ordinary-step">12</a>, terminates. No pseudo-effectivity hypothesis is required for this assertion about birational sequences.
  - Explanation: With empty boundary and terminal singularities, sequences of divisorial contractions and flips terminate, because divisorial steps are finite in number and a tail of flips contradicts termination of flips.
- **claim-scaling-program** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-scaling-construction)): Let $(X,\Delta)$ satisfy the hypotheses of Theorem <a href="#thm:finite" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:finite">2</a>. There is an ordinary negative-ray program, finite or infinite, $$(X,\Delta)=(X_0,\Delta_0)\dashrightarrow (X_1,\Delta_1)\dashrightarrow\cdots,$$ in the same global Weil-divisor $\mathbb Q$-factorial compact Kähler category. It stops when the ordinary adjoint is nef or a negative ray has a Mori fibre contraction. Put $$D_i=K_{X_i}+\Delta_i,\qquad L_i(t)=D_i+tH_i,\qquad H_i=H_{X_i},\qquad \lambda_{-1}=1.$$ At every stage before stopping there is a parameter $0<\lambda_i\leq\lambda_{i-1}$ and a contracted extremal ray $R_i$ such that $$D_i\cdot R_i<0,\qquad L_i(\lambda_i)\cdot R_i=0, \qquad L_i(\lambda_i)\text{ is nef}.$$ The generalized pairs $(X_i,\Delta_i+t\mathbf H)$ are gklt for $0\leq t\leq\lambda_{i-1}$. A birational step is crepant for the data at $t=\lambda_i$, and discrepancies do not decrease across that step for any $0\leq t\leq\lambda_i$.
  - Explanation: An ordinary negative-ray program with scaling by the fixed Kähler trace exists in the same category. Thresholds are nonincreasing, each step is crepant at its threshold, and generalized discrepancies do not decrease.
- **claim-thresholds-vanish** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-scaling-zero)): If the program of Proposition <a href="#prop:scaling-construction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:scaling-construction">22</a> is infinite, then $\lambda_i\to0$.
  - Explanation: If the scaling program is infinite, finiteness of marked weak models away from parameter zero forces the thresholds to tend to zero.
- **claim-stabilized-extraction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-terminal-limit)): Suppose the program of Proposition <a href="#prop:scaling-construction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:scaling-construction">22</a> is infinite. After discarding finitely many stages, there are proper bimeromorphic morphisms $$\mu_i:Y_i\longrightarrow X_i$$ with $Y_i$ strongly $\mathbb Q$-factorial and compact Kähler, and effective real boundaries $\Theta_i$, with the following properties.* 1. *The natural maps between the $Y_i$ are isomorphisms in codimension one, and $$\label{eq:extracted-nef-adjoint} Q_{i,Y_i}:=K_{Y_i}+\Theta_i+\lambda_iH_{Y_i} =\mu_i^*L_i(\lambda_i)$$ is nef. Each ordinary pair $(Y_i,\Theta_i)$ is klt and terminal.* 2. *On a fixed first model $Y=Y_{i_0}$, write $\Theta_{i,Y}$ for the strict transform of $\Theta_i$. These boundaries have a fixed finite containing support and decrease coefficientwise to an effective real boundary $\Theta_Y$. The ordinary pair $(Y,\Theta_Y)$ is klt and terminal.* 3. *The strict transform of $\Theta_Y$ on each $Y_i$ pushes forward to $\Delta_i$ under $\mu_i$.* *Here terminal means that every exceptional prime has log discrepancy strictly greater than one.
  - Explanation: For an infinite program, after discarding finitely many stages one extracts a stable finite set of low places on strongly factorial models. The boundaries decrease coefficientwise to an effective ordinary terminal boundary on a fixed model.
- **claim-limiting-nef-model** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-limiting-nef)): For these data there is a finite sequence of ordinary $(K_Y+\Theta_Y)$-flips $$Y\dashrightarrow W$$ such that $W$ is strongly $\mathbb Q$-factorial and compact Kähler, the transformed ordinary pair $(W,\Theta_W)$ is terminal, and $P:=K_W+\Theta_W$ is nef. In particular, $W$ is isomorphic in codimension one to every $Y_i$.
  - Explanation: A finite sequence of ordinary flips from the limiting pair reaches a strongly factorial terminal model with nef ordinary adjoint. Divisorial and fibre contractions are excluded by comparison with the nef extracted adjoints.
- **claim-rational-nef-polytope** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-rational-nef-polytope)): Let $W$ be a globally $\mathbb Q$-factorial compact Kähler fourfold with $K_W$ a $\mathbb Q$-line bundle. Let $B\ge0$ be a real boundary such that $(W,B)$ is klt and $K_W+B$ is nef. There are effective rational klt boundaries $B^1,\ldots,B^r$ and positive real numbers $u_1,\ldots,u_r$ such that $$\sum_{j=1}^r u_j=1,\qquad B=\sum_{j=1}^r u_jB^j,\qquad K_W+B^j\ \text{is nef for every }j.$$ The boundaries $B^j$ can be chosen on the positive support of $B$.
  - Explanation: A nef klt real boundary is a convex combination of effective rational klt boundaries whose adjoints are nef, shown by finitely many rational inequalities cutting out the nef locus.
- **claim-trivial-perturbation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-trivial-perturbation)): For the extracted data of Proposition <a href="#prop:terminal-limit" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:terminal-limit">24</a> and the model $W$ of Proposition <a href="#prop:limiting-nef" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:limiting-nef">25</a>, take $i$ sufficiently large. There is a finite generalized $Q_{i,W}$-program $W\dashrightarrow W_i$ consisting only of flips, with the following properties:* 1. *$Q_{i,W_i}$ is nef;* 2. *every step is trivial for the transformed class $P$ and for each $P^j$ in <a href="#eq:nef-decomposition" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:nef-decomposition">[eq:nef-decomposition]</a>;* 3. *the transformed $P^j$ are nef adjoints of ordinary rational klt pairs, and the fixed multiples $\ell_jP^j$ are line bundles;* 4. *every $Q_{i,W_i}$-trivial curve is $P_{W_i}$-trivial.
  - Explanation: For large index there is a finite program of flips for the perturbed generalized adjoint, trivial on the limiting adjoint and its rational pieces. It preserves their Cartier indices, and the trivial curves of the perturbed class are trivial for the limiting class.
- **claim-scaling-program-finite** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-scaling-finite)): Let $(X,\Delta)$ satisfy the hypotheses of Proposition <a href="#prop:scaling-construction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:scaling-construction">22</a>. The ordinary program constructed there, with scaling of the fixed initial Kähler b-part, is finite.
  - Explanation: The scaling program is finite: otherwise the limiting nef class descends to the base of an original flip and makes the negative adjoint trivial on its ray, a contradiction.
- **claim-pseudo-effectivity-preserved** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-pe-step)): Let $(T,B)\dashrightarrow(T',B')$ be an ordinary negative divisorial contraction or flip between normal compact Kähler klt pairs, with rational boundaries and rational line-bundle adjoints. Then $K_T+B$ is pseudo-effective if and only if $K_{T'}+B'$ is pseudo-effective.
  - Explanation: An ordinary negative divisorial contraction or flip preserves pseudo-effectivity of the adjoint in both directions, by pullback and descent over a common resolution.
- **claim-mori-not-pseudo-effective** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-mori-not-pe)): Let $g:Y\to S$ be a projective surjective morphism of normal compact Kähler varieties with $\dim S<\dim Y$. If a rational line bundle $D$ satisfies $-D$ $g$-ample, then $D$ is not pseudo-effective.
  - Explanation: A divisor whose negative is relatively ample over a lower-dimensional base cannot be pseudo-effective, since a positive current with a finite potential would give a nonnegative degree on a fibre curve.
- **claim-local-factoriality-counterexample** (counterexample, [source](#stmt-prop-local-convention)): There is a smooth projective fourfold $X$ with pseudo-effective, non-nef canonical divisor for which no ordinary $K_X$-negative program can reach a nef model while keeping every model analytically locally $\mathbb Q$-factorial. Its unique possible first negative birational contraction is divisorial. Its target is nevertheless globally factorial and admits a nef canonical divisor.
  - Explanation: A product of a nodal threefold resolution with an elliptic curve shows analytic local factoriality cannot replace the global convention. The only first negative contraction is divisorial with a globally factorial target admitting a nef canonical divisor, which is not locally factorial.

      "explanation": "Pullback of Bott-Chern classes along a fibre-connected proper surjection is injective. Its image consists of the classes of degree zero on vertical curves, and nefness is detected upstairs.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-positive-descent",
        "exact_quote": "Let $p:U\\to T$ be a resolution of a normal compact Kähler space, with $U$ compact Kähler. Let $\\alpha\\in H^{1,1}_{\\mathrm{BC}}(T)$ and let $E$ be a $p$-exceptional real divisor. If $p^*\\alpha+[E]$ is pseudo-effective, then $\\alpha$ is pseudo-effective. No sign condition on $E$ is needed. Pseudo-effective classes pull back to resolutions, and nef classes on $T$ are pseudo-effective.",
        "source_id": "PM-056.5"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-positive-descent",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-negativity",
        "exact_quote": "1.  *Let $f:T\\to Z$ be a proper bimeromorphic morphism of normal complex spaces and let $E$ be a real Cartier divisor such that $-E$ is $f$-nef. Then $E\\geq0$ if and only if $f_*E\\geq0$. Consequently an $f$-exceptional, $f$-numerically trivial real Cartier divisor is zero.*\n\n2.  *If, in addition, $f$ has connected projective fibres and $E\\geq0$ is $f$-anti-nef, then every fibre is either contained in $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Supp}}E$ or disjoint from $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Supp}}E$.*\n\n3.  *Consider a nontrivial small birational diagram $$T\\xrightarrow{\\ f\\ }Z\\xleftarrow{\\ f^+\\ }T^+$$ of normal compact spaces, with projective contractions on both sides. Let $B\\geq0$ be a real boundary, let $B^+$ be its strict transform, and suppose the ordinary adjoints $D=K_T+B$ and $D^+=K_{T^+}+B^+$ are real Cartier, with $-D$ relatively ample on the left and $D^+$ relatively ample on the right. The two exceptional loci have the same image $A\\subset Z$. On a common resolution $p:V\\to T$, $q:V\\to T^+$, $$\\label{eq:ordinary-flip-comparison}\n     p^*D-q^*D^+=F,\\qquad F\\geq0,$$ where $F$ is an actual divisor exceptional over both models. For every prime divisor $P$ over these spaces, $$a(P,T^+,B^+)\\geq a(P,T,B),$$ and the inequality is strict if the centre of $P$ on either side is contained in that side’s exceptional locus.*\n\n4.  *For the diagram in <span class=\"upright\">(iii)</span>, if $\\dim T=4$, then $\\dim A\\leq1$ and $$\\dim\\mathop{\\mathrm{Exc}}(f)+\\dim\\mathop{\\mathrm{Exc}}(f^+)\\geq3.$$*\n\n5.  *The discrepancy conclusions in <span class=\"upright\">(iii)</span> also hold for generalized pairs with the same fixed b-nef datum and transformed boundaries in a projective small diagram, provided the source log class has negative degree on every curve contracted on that side, and the target log class has positive degree on every curve contracted on the other side. They hold likewise for a projective divisorial contraction $f:T\\to T'$ when the target boundary is the pushforward, the b-datum is unchanged, and the source log class has negative degree on every $f$-vertical curve. In this divisorial case strictness holds at every prime whose source centre lies in $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Exc}}(f)$. This part also applies when the fixed b-datum is nef only over a specified base.",
        "source_id": "PM-056.5"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-class-trace",
        "exact_quote": "Let $T$ be a normal compact Kähler space with rational singularities, marked birationally to $X$. Suppose that on a common smooth Kähler resolution $p:V\\to T$, $q:V\\to X$ there are a class $H_T\\in H^{1,1}_{\\mathrm{BC}}(T)$ and an actual $p$-exceptional real divisor $J_T$ satisfying $$\\label{eq:class-trace}\n p^*H_T=q^*H+[J_T].$$ Then $J_T\\geq0$. The class $H_T$ and the divisor $J_T$ are unique, and the correction on a higher resolution is the pullback of $J_T$.*\n\n\n*For an effective boundary $B_T$ with real Cartier ordinary adjoint, the generalized structure boundary for the nef datum $t\\mathbf H$ is $$\\label{eq:trace-boundary}\n B_{T,V}(t)=p^*(K_T+B_T)-K_V+tJ_T,\\qquad t\\geq0.$$ In particular, decreasing $t$ increases every generalized log discrepancy. If the generalized pair is gklt, its ordinary pair is klt; if all its exceptional log discrepancies exceed one, the ordinary pair is terminal.*\n\n*Conversely, suppose $t>0$ and a generalized pair with boundary $B_T$ and this fixed datum $t\\mathbf H$ is given by <a href=\"#eq:generalized-structure\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:generalized-structure\">[eq:generalized-structure]</a>, with log class $L$ and real Cartier ordinary adjoint. Then <a href=\"#eq:class-trace\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:class-trace\">[eq:class-trace]</a> holds with $$H_T=\\frac{L-[K_T+B_T]}{t}.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-056.5"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-class-trace",

The class $H_T$ and the divisor $J_T$ are unique, and the correction on a higher resolution is the pullback of $J_T$.*\n\n\n*For an effective boundary $B_T$ with real Cartier ordinary adjoint, the generalized structure boundary for the nef datum $t\\mathbf H$ is $$\\label{eq:trace-boundary}\n B_{T,V}(t)=p^*(K_T+B_T)-K_V+tJ_T,\\qquad t\\geq0.$$ In particular, decreasing $t$ increases every generalized log discrepancy. If the generalized pair is gklt, its ordinary pair is klt; if all its exceptional log discrepancies exceed one, the ordinary pair is terminal.*\n\n*Conversely, suppose $t>0$ and a generalized pair with boundary $B_T$ and this fixed datum $t\\mathbf H$ is given by <a href=\"#eq:generalized-structure\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:generalized-structure\">[eq:generalized-structure]</a>, with log class $L$ and real Cartier ordinary adjoint. Then <a href=\"#eq:class-trace\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:class-trace\">[eq:class-trace]</a> holds with $$H_T=\\frac{L-[K_T+B_T]}{t}.$$",

If the generalized pair is gklt, its ordinary pair is klt; if all its exceptional log discrepancies exceed one, the ordinary pair is terminal.*\n\n*Conversely, suppose $t>0$ and a generalized pair with boundary $B_T$ and this fixed datum $t\\mathbf H$ is given by <a href=\"#eq:generalized-structure\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:generalized-structure\">[eq:generalized-structure]</a>, with log class $L$ and real Cartier ordinary adjoint.         "anchor": "stmt-cor-big-trace",
        "exact_quote": "Under the hypotheses of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:class-trace\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:class-trace\">6</a>, the actual trace $\\mathbf H_T$ has local plurisubharmonic potentials and represents $H_T$. The class $H_T$ is big.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-small-comparison",
        "exact_quote": "Let $(T,B_T+\\mathbf M)$ and $(T',B_{T'}+\\mathbf M)$ be generalized pairs with the same b-nef datum, whose log classes are $L_T$ and $L_{T'}$. Suppose the marked birational map $T\\dashrightarrow T'$ extracts no divisors and $B_{T'}$ is the pushforward of $B_T$. On a common sufficiently high resolution $p:V\\to T$, $q:V\\to T'$, put $$F=B_{T,V}-B_{T',V}.$$ Then $F$ is an actual divisor exceptional over $T'$, and $$\\label{eq:small-comparison}\n p^*L_T-q^*L_{T'}=[F],\\qquad\n \\operatorname{coeff}_P F\n =a(P,T',B_{T'}+\\mathbf M)-a(P,T,B_T+\\mathbf M).$$ If the map is small, $F$ is exceptional over both models. In this case nefness of $L_{T'}$ implies $F\\geq0$, and nefness of both log classes implies $F=0$. These conclusions also hold relatively over a common base. Even without smallness, equality of the pullback classes in <a href=\"#eq:small-comparison\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:small-comparison\">[eq:small-comparison]</a> implies equality of the structure boundaries.",
        "source_id": "PM-056.5"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-small-comparison",
          "exact_quote": "Let $(T,B_T+\\mathbf M)$ and $(T',B_{T'}+\\mathbf M)$ be generalized pairs with the same b-nef datum, whose log classes are $L_T$ and $L_{T'}$. Suppose the marked birational map $T\\dashrightarrow T'$ extracts no divisors and $B_{T'}$ is the pushforward of $B_T$. On a common sufficiently high resolution $p:V\\to T$, $q:V\\to T'$, put $$F=B_{T,V}-B_{T',V}.$$ Then $F$ is an actual divisor exceptional over $T'$, and $$\\label{eq:small-comparison}\n p^*L_T-q^*L_{T'}=[F],\\qquad\n \\operatorname{coeff}_P F\n =a(P,T',B_{T'}+\\mathbf M)-a(P,T,B_T+\\mathbf M).$$ If the map is small, $F$ is exceptional over both models. In this case nefness of $L_{T'}$ implies $F\\geq0$, and nefness of both log classes implies $F=0$. These conclusions also hold relatively over a common base. Even without smallness, equality of the pullback classes in <a href=\"#eq:small-comparison\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:small-comparison\">[eq:small-comparison]</a> implies equality of the structure boundaries.",
          "source_id": "PM-056.5"
        }

      "exact_statement": "Let $(T,B_T+\\mathbf M)$ and $(T',B_{T'}+\\mathbf M)$ be generalized pairs with the same b-nef datum, whose log classes are $L_T$ and $L_{T'}$. In this case nefness of $L_{T'}$ implies $F\\geq0$, and nefness of both log classes implies $F=0$. These conclusions also hold relatively over a common base. Even without smallness, equality of the pullback classes in <a href=\"#eq:small-comparison\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:small-comparison\">[eq:small-comparison]</a> implies equality of the structure boundaries.",
      "explanation": "For two generalized pairs sharing a b-nef datum, the difference of log classes pulled back to a common resolution is an actual divisor whose coefficients are discrepancy differences. For small maps nefness forces it to be effective or zero.",

      "hypotheses": [

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-descent",
        "exact_quote": "Let $f:T\\to Z$ be a projective bimeromorphic contraction of normal complex spaces, and let $L$ be a line bundle numerically trivial over $Z$. Suppose that every point of $Z$ has a neighbourhood $U$ on which there is an ordinary rational klt pair $(f^{-1}U,C_U)$ with rational adjoint and $$L-(K_{f^{-1}U}+C_U)\\quad\\text{$f$-nef over $U$}.$$ Then $f_*L$ is a line bundle and the evaluation map is an isomorphism $$\\label{eq:integral-descent}\n f^*f_*L\\simeq L.$$ In particular the conclusion applies if the local ordinary adjoints are relatively antiample. It also applies to a global ordinary rational klt pair $(T,C)$ whenever $L-(K_T+C)$ is $f$-nef. Clearing one denominator therefore descends a rational line bundle as an actual rational line bundle.",
        "source_id": "PM-056.5"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-descent",
          "exact_quote": "Let $f:T\\to Z$ be a projective bimeromorphic contraction of normal complex spaces, and let $L$ be a line bundle numerically trivial over $Z$. It also applies to a global ordinary rational klt pair $(T,C)$ whenever $L-(K_T+C)$ is $f$-nef. Clearing one denominator therefore descends a rational line bundle as an actual rational line bundle.",
          "source_id": "PM-056.5"
        }
      ],

It also applies to a global ordinary rational klt pair $(T,C)$ whenever $L-(K_T+C)$ is $f$-nef. Clearing one denominator therefore descends a rational line bundle as an actual rational line bundle.",
      "explanation": "A line bundle numerically trivial over a projective bimeromorphic contraction, with a locally nef difference from a rational klt adjoint, descends as an actual line bundle with no extra tensor power.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-absolute-support",
        "exact_quote": "Let $(T,B)$ be an ordinary rational klt pair on a normal compact Kähler space. Assume that $B$ is $\\mathbb Q$-Cartier and that $D=K_T+B$ is a rational line bundle. If $D$ is not nef, $\\overline{\\mathrm{NA}}(T)$ has a $D$-negative extremal ray. Every such ray $R$ is generated by a rational curve and has a nonempty relatively open set $\\mathcal U_R\\subset R^\\perp$ of classes satisfying $$\\label{eq:absolute-support}\n \\alpha\\text{ is nef},\\qquad\n \\overline{\\mathrm{NA}}(T)\\cap\\alpha^\\perp=R,\\qquad\n \\alpha-c_1(D)\\text{ is K\\\"ahler}.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-056.5"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-absolute-support",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-relative-positivity",
        "exact_quote": "Let $f:T\\to Z$ be a proper morphism of compact complex spaces, with $T$ and $Z$ Kähler. Suppose that $L$ is a rational line bundle and $$c_1(L)=\\kappa-f^*\\gamma,$$ where $\\kappa$ is Kähler on $T$ and $\\gamma$ is a smooth real Bott–Chern class on $Z$. Then $L$ is $f$-ample and $f$ is projective. Conversely, a normal space projective over a compact Kähler space is compact Kähler if it has a global relatively ample line bundle.",
        "source_id": "PM-056.5"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-relative-positivity",
          "exact_quote": "Let $f:T\\to Z$ be a proper morphism of compact complex spaces, with $T$ and $Z$ Kähler. Then $L$ is $f$-ample and $f$ is projective. Conversely, a normal space projective over a compact Kähler space is compact Kähler if it has a global relatively ample line bundle.",
          "source_id": "PM-056.5"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $f:T\\to Z$ be a proper morphism of compact complex spaces, with $T$ and $Z$ Kähler. Then $L$ is $f$-ample and $f$ is projective. Conversely, a normal space projective over a compact Kähler space is compact Kähler if it has a global relatively ample line bundle.",
      "explanation": "A class of the form Kähler minus a pullback from the base makes the line bundle relatively ample and the map projective. Conversely a relatively ample bundle over a Kähler base gives a Kähler total space.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-ordinary-step",
        "exact_quote": "Let $(T,B)$ be an ordinary rational klt pair on a normal compact Kähler space, globally $\\mathbb Q$-factorial in the Weil-divisor sense, with canonical sheaf a rational line bundle. Put $D=K_T+B$. Every $D$-negative extremal ray $R\\subset\\overline{\\mathrm{NA}}(T)$ has a projective contraction $f:T\\to Z$ with connected fibres and normal compact Kähler target, such that $$\\label{eq:ordinary-negative-bc}\n f^*H^{1,1}_{\\mathrm{BC}}(Z)=R^\\perp,\\qquad\n \\rho(T/Z)=1,\\qquad -D\\text{ is }f\\text{-ample}.$$ Here $\\rho$ is the rank of global line-bundle degrees on contracted curves. The target has rational singularities and $R^j f_*\\mathcal O_T=0$ for $j>0$. If $\\dim Z<\\dim T$, this is a Mori fibre space. Otherwise $f$ is bimeromorphic, and precisely one of the following occurs.*\n\n\n1.  *The exceptional locus is one prime divisor $E$. Put $T'=Z$, $B'=f_*B$ and $D'=K_{T'}+B'$. Then $$\\label{eq:ordinary-divisorial-difference}\n     D=f^*D'+eE\\qquad(e>0).$$*\n\n\n\n2.  *The contraction is small. For an adjoint Cartier index $r>0$, the intrinsic algebra $$\\label{eq:ordinary-flip-algebra}\n     \\mathcal R_r=\\bigoplus_{m\\geq0}f_*\\mathcal O_T(mrD)$$ is locally finitely generated, and $T'=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Proj}}_Z\\mathcal R_r$ is the ordinary flip. It has a small projective morphism $f^+:T'\\to Z$ with connected fibres. Put $B'=\\phi_*B$ and $D'=K_{T'}+B'$, where $\\phi:T\\dashrightarrow T'$ is the induced small map. For a sufficiently divisible choice of $r$ there is an actual isomorphism $$\\label{eq:ordinary-tautological}\n     \\mathcal O_{\\mathop{\\mathrm{Proj}}_Z\\mathcal R_r}(1)\\simeq\\mathcal O_{T'}(rD').$$ In particular $D'$ is a rational line bundle and is $f^+$-ample. Passing to a sufficiently divisible Veronese leaves $T'$ unchanged.*\n\n*In both birational cases, $T'$ is compact Kähler and globally Weil-divisor $\\mathbb Q$-factorial, its canonical sheaf is a rational line bundle, and $(T',B')$ is klt. Strong global $\\mathbb Q$-factoriality is preserved when imposed on $T$. A supplied global canonical Weil divisor is transported to a canonical Weil divisor on $T'$. No prime divisor is extracted. All log discrepancies weakly increase; the increase is strict for every original prime contracted by the step, and, for a flip, for every place centred in either exceptional locus.",
        "source_id": "PM-056.5"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-ordinary-step",
          "exact_quote": "Let $(T,B)$ be an ordinary rational klt pair on a normal compact Kähler space, globally $\\mathbb Q$-factorial in the Weil-divisor sense, with canonical sheaf a rational line bundle. The target has rational singularities and $R^j f_*\\mathcal O_T=0$ for $j>0$. If $\\dim Z<\\dim T$, this is a Mori fibre space. Otherwise $f$ is bimeromorphic, and precisely one of the following occurs.*\n\n\n1.  *The exceptional locus is one prime divisor $E$. Put $T'=Z$, $B'=f_*B$ and $D'=K_{T'}+B'$. Then $$\\label{eq:ordinary-divisorial-difference}\n     D=f^*D'+eE\\qquad(e>0).$$*\n\n\n\n2.  *The contraction is small. For an adjoint Cartier index $r>0$, the intrinsic algebra $$\\label{eq:ordinary-flip-algebra}\n     \\mathcal R_r=\\bigoplus_{m\\geq0}f_*\\mathcal O_T(mrD)$$ is locally finitely generated, and $T'=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Proj}}_Z\\mathcal R_r$ is the ordinary flip. It has a small projective morphism $f^+:T'\\to Z$ with connected fibres. Put $B'=\\phi_*B$ and $D'=K_{T'}+B'$, where $\\phi:T\\dashrightarrow T'$ is the induced small map. For a sufficiently divisible choice of $r$ there is an actual isomorphism $$\\label{eq:ordinary-tautological}\n     \\mathcal O_{\\mathop{\\mathrm{Proj}}_Z\\mathcal R_r}(1)\\simeq\\mathcal O_{T'}(rD').$$ In particular $D'$ is a rational line bundle and is $f^+$-ample. Passing to a sufficiently divisible Veronese leaves $T'$ unchanged.*\n\n*In both birational cases, $T'$ is compact Kähler and globally Weil-divisor $\\mathbb Q$-factorial, its canonical sheaf is a rational line bundle, and $(T',B')$ is klt. Strong global $\\mathbb Q$-factoriality is preserved when imposed on $T$. A supplied global canonical Weil divisor is transported to a canonical Weil divisor on $T'$. No prime divisor is extracted.       "exact_statement": "Let $(T,B)$ be an ordinary rational klt pair on a normal compact Kähler space, globally $\\mathbb Q$-factorial in the Weil-divisor sense, with canonical sheaf a rational line bundle. Passing to a sufficiently divisible Veronese leaves $T'$ unchanged.*\n\n*In both birational cases, $T'$ is compact Kähler and globally Weil-divisor $\\mathbb Q$-factorial, its canonical sheaf is a rational line bundle, and $(T',B')$ is klt.       "explanation": "Every negative extremal ray has a projective contraction with the stated Bott-Chern and rank properties.         "anchor": "stmt-lem-class-transport",
        "exact_quote": "For a birational step of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:ordinary-step\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:ordinary-step\">12</a>, let $f':T'\\to Z$ mean $f^+$ in the small case and $\\mathrm{id}_Z$ in the divisorial case. Every $\\theta\\in H^{1,1}_{\\mathrm{BC}}(T)$ has a unique expression $\\theta=f^*\\eta+s\\,c_1(D)$, with $\\eta\\in H^{1,1}_{\\mathrm{BC}}(Z)$ and $s\\in\\mathbb R$. Define $$\\label{eq:class-transport}\n \\theta'= (f')^*\\eta+s\\,c_1(D').$$ On a common resolution, $p^*\\theta-q^*\\theta'$ is the class of an actual divisor exceptional over $T'$. This rule sends the class of a global Weil divisor to that of its transform or pushforward. Moreover, it propagates the fixed-nef-part trace relation of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:class-trace\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:class-trace\">6</a>, with an actual effective exceptional correction on the new model. The same conclusions hold for ordinary real-boundary flips on strongly globally $\\mathbb Q$-factorial compact Kähler spaces constructed by (Hacon and Xie 2026, Proposition 4.2) [[HX2026](#ref-HX2026)].",
        "source_id": "PM-056.5"
      },

Define $$\\label{eq:class-transport}\n \\theta'= (f')^*\\eta+s\\,c_1(D').$$ On a common resolution, $p^*\\theta-q^*\\theta'$ is the class of an actual divisor exceptional over $T'$. This rule sends the class of a global Weil divisor to that of its transform or pushforward. Moreover, it propagates the fixed-nef-part trace relation of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:class-trace\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:class-trace\">6</a>, with an actual effective exceptional correction on the new model.       "exact_statement": "For a birational step of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:ordinary-step\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:ordinary-step\">12</a>, let $f':T'\\to Z$ mean $f^+$ in the small case and $\\mathrm{id}_Z$ in the divisorial case.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-cycle-loss",
        "exact_quote": "Let $X\\dashrightarrow Y$ be a bimeromorphic transformation of compact Kähler spaces, represented by a proper bimeromorphic diagram. Suppose it identifies $$U=X\\setminus A\\simeq Y\\setminus B,$$ where $A$ and $B$ are closed analytic subspaces and $\\dim B<k$. Then there is a surjection $$\\mathcal C_k(X)\\longrightarrow\\mathcal C_k(Y).$$ If $A$ contains an irreducible compact analytic $k$-dimensional subspace, then $c_k(X)>c_k(Y)$.*\n\n*Consequently, in a sequence of bimeromorphic transformations of $n$-dimensional compact Kähler spaces which extract no prime divisors, only finitely many transformations contract a prime divisor. A small transformation preserves $c_{n-1}$.",
        "source_id": "PM-056.5"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-cycle-loss",
          "exact_quote": "Let $X\\dashrightarrow Y$ be a bimeromorphic transformation of compact Kähler spaces, represented by a proper bimeromorphic diagram. Suppose it identifies $$U=X\\setminus A\\simeq Y\\setminus B,$$ where $A$ and $B$ are closed analytic subspaces and $\\dim B<k$. Then there is a surjection $$\\mathcal C_k(X)\\longrightarrow\\mathcal C_k(Y).$$ If $A$ contains an irreducible compact analytic $k$-dimensional subspace, then $c_k(X)>c_k(Y)$.*\n\n*Consequently, in a sequence of bimeromorphic transformations of $n$-dimensional compact Kähler spaces which extract no prime divisors, only finitely many transformations contract a prime divisor. A small transformation preserves $c_{n-1}$.",
          "source_id": "PM-056.5"
        }

      "exact_statement": "Let $X\\dashrightarrow Y$ be a bimeromorphic transformation of compact Kähler spaces, represented by a proper bimeromorphic diagram. Then there is a surjection $$\\mathcal C_k(X)\\longrightarrow\\mathcal C_k(Y).$$ If $A$ contains an irreducible compact analytic $k$-dimensional subspace, then $c_k(X)>c_k(Y)$.*\n\n*Consequently, in a sequence of bimeromorphic transformations of $n$-dimensional compact Kähler spaces which extract no prime divisors, only finitely many transformations contract a prime divisor. A small transformation preserves $c_{n-1}$.",
      "explanation": "Cycle spans surject under bimeromorphic maps that are isomorphisms off small sets, and drop strictly when a compact subspace of the right dimension is contracted. Hence only finitely many steps contract a prime divisor, while small steps preserve the codimension-one count.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-low-places",
        "exact_quote": "Let $(X,B+\\mathbf M)$ be a generalized pair on a compact normal complex space, with effective real boundary of finite support and nef b-$(1,1)$ data descending to a model projective over $X$. Fix a projective log resolution $p:W\\to X$ carrying those data.*\n\n1.  *If the pair is generalized klt, there is $\\epsilon>0$ such that every prime divisor over $X$ has log discrepancy at least $\\epsilon$, and only finitely many exceptional places have $$a(E;X,B+\\mathbf M)<1+\\epsilon.$$*\n\n2.  *If the pair is generalized terminal and $b=\\max(\\{0\\}\\cup\\{\\operatorname{coeff}_D B\\})$, there are only finitely many exceptional places with $$a(E;X,B+\\mathbf M)<2-b.$$*\n\n*In each assertion the finite set of exceptional places can be represented simultaneously by prime divisors on a smooth model projective over $X$. In particular, any prescribed finite set of exceptional places with $a(E;X,B+\\mathbf M)\\leq 1$ for a generalized klt pair has such a realization. This conclusion does not use an extraction or termination theorem.",
        "source_id": "PM-056.5"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-low-places",
          "exact_quote": "Let $(X,B+\\mathbf M)$ be a generalized pair on a compact normal complex space, with effective real boundary of finite support and nef b-$(1,1)$ data descending to a model projective over $X$. In particular, any prescribed finite set of exceptional places with $a(E;X,B+\\mathbf M)\\leq 1$ for a generalized klt pair has such a realization. This conclusion does not use an extraction or termination theorem.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-terminal-codim-two",
        "exact_quote": "Let $(T,\\Phi)$ be an ordinary klt pair on a normal complex fourfold, with effective real boundary, $K_T$ a $\\mathbb Q$-line bundle, and $a(E;T,\\Phi)>1$ for every exceptional prime divisor $E$ over $T$. Then $T$ is smooth in codimension two.",
        "source_id": "PM-056.5"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-terminal-codim-two",
          "exact_quote": "Let $(T,\\Phi)$ be an ordinary klt pair on a normal complex fourfold, with effective real boundary, $K_T$ a $\\mathbb Q$-line bundle, and $a(E;T,\\Phi)>1$ for every exceptional prime divisor $E$ over $T$. Then $T$ is smooth in codimension two.",
          "source_id": "PM-056.5"
        }

      "exact_statement": "Let $(T,\\Phi)$ be an ordinary klt pair on a normal complex fourfold, with effective real boundary, $K_T$ a $\\mathbb Q$-line bundle, and $a(E;T,\\Phi)>1$ for every exceptional prime divisor $E$ over $T$. Then $T$ is smooth in codimension two.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-terminal-low-centres",
        "exact_quote": "Let $(T,\\Phi)$ be an ordinary klt pair on a compact normal complex fourfold, where $\\Phi\\geq0$ is real and $a(E;T,\\Phi)>1$ for every exceptional prime divisor $E$ over $T$. Apart from finitely many exceptional places, every exceptional place with $a(E;T,\\Phi)<2$ has centre a surface $S\\subset T$ with the following properties: $T$ and the reduced support of $\\Phi$ are generically smooth along $S$, exactly one positive boundary component contains $S$, and, if its coefficient is $b$, then $$a(E;T,\\Phi)\\geq 2-b.$$ All places above such a surface are allowed in this conclusion, not just the exceptional divisor of its first blowup.",
        "source_id": "PM-056.5"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-terminal-low-centres",
          "exact_quote": "Let $(T,\\Phi)$ be an ordinary klt pair on a compact normal complex fourfold, where $\\Phi\\geq0$ is real and $a(E;T,\\Phi)>1$ for every exceptional prime divisor $E$ over $T$.           "source_id": "PM-056.5"
        }
      ],

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-terminal-real-four",
        "exact_quote": "Let $(T_0,\\Phi_0)$ be an ordinary klt pair on a normal globally $\\mathbb Q$-factorial compact Kähler fourfold, with $K_{T_0}$ a $\\mathbb Q$-line bundle and effective real boundary. Suppose $a(E;T_0,\\Phi_0)>1$ for every exceptional prime divisor $E$ over $T_0$. Every sequence of ordinary $(K_{T_i}+\\Phi_i)$-flips starting at this pair terminates, provided all models are normal globally $\\mathbb Q$-factorial compact Kähler fourfolds, each $K_{T_i}$ is a $\\mathbb Q$-line bundle, and each flip is given by projective small contractions $$T_i\\xrightarrow{\\ f_i\\ }Z_i\n \\xleftarrow{\\ f_i^+\\ }T_{i+1}$$ with $-(K_{T_i}+\\Phi_i)$ relatively ample for $f_i$ and $K_{T_{i+1}}+\\Phi_{i+1}$ relatively ample for $f_i^+$. Here $\\Phi_{i+1}$ is the strict transform of $\\Phi_i$. No pseudo-effectivity or bigness assumption is required.",
        "source_id": "PM-056.5"
      },
      "evidence": [

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-scaling-construction",
        "exact_quote": "Let $(X,\\Delta)$ satisfy the hypotheses of Theorem <a href=\"#thm:finite\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:finite\">2</a>. There is an ordinary negative-ray program, finite or infinite, $$(X,\\Delta)=(X_0,\\Delta_0)\\dashrightarrow\n (X_1,\\Delta_1)\\dashrightarrow\\cdots,$$ in the same global Weil-divisor $\\mathbb Q$-factorial compact Kähler category. It stops when the ordinary adjoint is nef or a negative ray has a Mori fibre contraction. Put $$D_i=K_{X_i}+\\Delta_i,\\qquad\n L_i(t)=D_i+tH_i,\\qquad H_i=H_{X_i},\\qquad \\lambda_{-1}=1.$$ At every stage before stopping there is a parameter $0<\\lambda_i\\leq\\lambda_{i-1}$ and a contracted extremal ray $R_i$ such that $$D_i\\cdot R_i<0,\\qquad L_i(\\lambda_i)\\cdot R_i=0,\n \\qquad L_i(\\lambda_i)\\text{ is nef}.$$ The generalized pairs $(X_i,\\Delta_i+t\\mathbf H)$ are gklt for $0\\leq t\\leq\\lambda_{i-1}$. A birational step is crepant for the data at $t=\\lambda_i$, and discrepancies do not decrease across that step for any $0\\leq t\\leq\\lambda_i$.",
        "source_id": "PM-056.5"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-scaling-construction",

It stops when the ordinary adjoint is nef or a negative ray has a Mori fibre contraction. Put $$D_i=K_{X_i}+\\Delta_i,\\qquad\n L_i(t)=D_i+tH_i,\\qquad H_i=H_{X_i},\\qquad \\lambda_{-1}=1.$$ At every stage before stopping there is a parameter $0<\\lambda_i\\leq\\lambda_{i-1}$ and a contracted extremal ray $R_i$ such that $$D_i\\cdot R_i<0,\\qquad L_i(\\lambda_i)\\cdot R_i=0,\n \\qquad L_i(\\lambda_i)\\text{ is nef}.$$ The generalized pairs $(X_i,\\Delta_i+t\\mathbf H)$ are gklt for $0\\leq t\\leq\\lambda_{i-1}$. A birational step is crepant for the data at $t=\\lambda_i$, and discrepancies do not decrease across that step for any $0\\leq t\\leq\\lambda_i$.",

      "exact_statement": "Let $(X,\\Delta)$ satisfy the hypotheses of Theorem <a href=\"#thm:finite\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:finite\">2</a>.         "anchor": "stmt-prop-terminal-limit",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose the program of Proposition <a href=\"#prop:scaling-construction\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:scaling-construction\">22</a> is infinite. After discarding finitely many stages, there are proper bimeromorphic morphisms $$\\mu_i:Y_i\\longrightarrow X_i$$ with $Y_i$ strongly $\\mathbb Q$-factorial and compact Kähler, and effective real boundaries $\\Theta_i$, with the following properties.*\n\n\n1.  *The natural maps between the $Y_i$ are isomorphisms in codimension one, and $$\\label{eq:extracted-nef-adjoint}\n     Q_{i,Y_i}:=K_{Y_i}+\\Theta_i+\\lambda_iH_{Y_i}\n           =\\mu_i^*L_i(\\lambda_i)$$ is nef. Each ordinary pair $(Y_i,\\Theta_i)$ is klt and terminal.*\n\n2.  *On a fixed first model $Y=Y_{i_0}$, write $\\Theta_{i,Y}$ for the strict transform of $\\Theta_i$. These boundaries have a fixed finite containing support and decrease coefficientwise to an effective real boundary $\\Theta_Y$. The ordinary pair $(Y,\\Theta_Y)$ is klt and terminal.*\n\n3.  *The strict transform of $\\Theta_Y$ on each $Y_i$ pushes forward to $\\Delta_i$ under $\\mu_i$.*\n\n*Here terminal means that every exceptional prime has log discrepancy strictly greater than one.",
        "source_id": "PM-056.5"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

These boundaries have a fixed finite containing support and decrease coefficientwise to an effective real boundary $\\Theta_Y$. The ordinary pair $(Y,\\Theta_Y)$ is klt and terminal.*\n\n3.  *The strict transform of $\\Theta_Y$ on each $Y_i$ pushes forward to $\\Delta_i$ under $\\mu_i$.*\n\n*Here terminal means that every exceptional prime has log discrepancy strictly greater than one.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-rational-nef-polytope",
        "exact_quote": "Let $W$ be a globally $\\mathbb Q$-factorial compact Kähler fourfold with $K_W$ a $\\mathbb Q$-line bundle. Let $B\\ge0$ be a real boundary such that $(W,B)$ is klt and $K_W+B$ is nef. There are effective rational klt boundaries $B^1,\\ldots,B^r$ and positive real numbers $u_1,\\ldots,u_r$ such that $$\\sum_{j=1}^r u_j=1,\\qquad\n B=\\sum_{j=1}^r u_jB^j,\\qquad\n K_W+B^j\\ \\text{is nef for every }j.$$ The boundaries $B^j$ can be chosen on the positive support of $B$.",
        "source_id": "PM-056.5"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-rational-nef-polytope",
          "exact_quote": "Let $W$ be a globally $\\mathbb Q$-factorial compact Kähler fourfold with $K_W$ a $\\mathbb Q$-line bundle. Let $B\\ge0$ be a real boundary such that $(W,B)$ is klt and $K_W+B$ is nef. There are effective rational klt boundaries $B^1,\\ldots,B^r$ and positive real numbers $u_1,\\ldots,u_r$ such that $$\\sum_{j=1}^r u_j=1,\\qquad\n B=\\sum_{j=1}^r u_jB^j,\\qquad\n K_W+B^j\\ \\text{is nef for every }j.$$ The boundaries $B^j$ can be chosen on the positive support of $B$.",
          "source_id": "PM-056.5"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $W$ be a globally $\\mathbb Q$-factorial compact Kähler fourfold with $K_W$ a $\\mathbb Q$-line bundle. Let $B\\ge0$ be a real boundary such that $(W,B)$ is klt and $K_W+B$ is nef. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **globally Weil Q-factorial** ([source](#stmt-def-strong-factoriality)): . A normal compact space $X$ is *globally Weil $\mathbb Q$-factorial* if every prime Weil divisor defined on the whole of $X$ is $\mathbb Q$-Cartier and some positive reflexive power of its canonical sheaf $\omega_X$ is a line bundle.
- **globally strongly Q-factorial** ([source](#stmt-def-strong-factoriality)): It is *globally strongly $\mathbb Q$-factorial* if every coherent rank-one reflexive sheaf $\mathcal F$ on $X$ has an invertible positive reflexive power $\mathcal F^{[m]}=(\mathcal F^{\otimes m})^{**}$.
- **rational line bundle** ([source](#pm-h-3)): A rational line bundle is an element of $\mathop{\mathrm{Pic}}(X)\otimes_{\mathbb Z}\mathbb Q$.
- **log discrepancy** ([source](#pm-h-3)): We use *log discrepancies* throughout: $$a(E;X,\Delta) =1+\operatorname{coeff}_E\bigl(K_W-p^*(K_X+\Delta)\bigr)$$ on a smooth model $p:W\to X$ carrying $E$.
- **klt pair** ([source](#pm-h-3)): A pair is klt when all these numbers are positive.
- **terminal pair** ([source](#pm-h-3)): It is terminal when it is klt and they are greater than one for every exceptional divisor over $X$.
- **nef and pseudo-effective classes** ([source](#pm-h-3)): A class is *nef* if it belongs to the closure of the Kähler cone, and *pseudo-effective* if it contains a closed positive $(1,1)$-current with local potentials.
- **big class** ([source](#pm-h-3)): A class is *big* if it contains a Kähler current, meaning a closed current that dominates a positive multiple of a Kähler form.
- **ordinary D-negative step** ([source](#pm-h-3)): An *ordinary $D$-negative step* is a projective divisorial contraction, or the flip of a projective small contraction, of a $D$-negative extremal ray of $\overline{\mathrm{NA}}(X)$.
- **Mori fibre space** ([source](#pm-h-3)): A *Mori fibre space* has the same contraction properties and a base of strictly smaller dimension.
- **Fujiki class C** ([source](#pm-h-7)): A space is in *Fujiki’s class $\mathcal C$* if it is bimeromorphic to a compact Kähler manifold.
- **gklt and glc pairs** ([source](#pm-h-9)): The pair is generalized klt, or *gklt*, if all these discrepancies are positive, and generalized log canonical, or *glc*, if they are all nonnegative.

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-mori-base-dim | dimension of the Mori fibre space base | < | $4$ | non-pseudo-effective adjoint alternative |
| q-relative-picard-rank | relative Picard rank of the Mori fibration | = | $1$ | final contraction onto the base |
| q-exceptional-image-dim | dimension of the common exceptional image of a small flip | ≤ | $1$ | fourfold case |
| q-exceptional-dim-sum | sum of dimensions of the flipping and flipped loci | ≥ | $3$ | fourfold case |
| q-graph-vertical-degree | degree of the difference divisor on a vertical curve of the graph | < | $0$ | every curve vertical over the base |
| q-terminal-discrepancy-threshold | log discrepancy of exceptional divisors on a terminal pair | > | $1$ | every exceptional divisor over the space |
| q-low-place-cutoff-terminal | log discrepancy cutoff for the finite set of low places (terminal case) | < | $2-b$ | b is the largest boundary coefficient, or zero |
| q-amplification-gap | amplification integer times the positive gap | > | $8$ | choice of the integer m in the perturbation |
| q-curve-degree-lower | degree of a vertex adjoint on a minimal extremal curve | ≥ | $-8$ | vertex adjoints and a curve minimizing on an extremal ray |
| q-weights-sum | sum of the convex decomposition weights | = | $1$ | nef klt real boundary |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-trace-and-descent** [claim](#stmt-lem-pullback-image) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-pullback-image): Cite pullback injectivity and nef descent for fibre-connected surjections between spaces with rational singularities. -> Bott-Chern pullback description used throughout
- **step-exceptional-descent** [claim](#stmt-lem-positive-descent) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-positive-descent): Extend plurisubharmonic potentials across codimension-two sets and normal singularities, then pull back and take weak limits. -> descent of pseudo-effectivity along resolutions
- **step-negativity** [claim](#stmt-lem-negativity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-negativity): Analytic negativity lemma, fibre chains of curves, and the effective difference divisor on the normalized graph. -> strict discrepancy comparison across flips and contractions
- **step-trace-relation** [claim](#stmt-lem-class-trace) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-class-trace): Apply negativity to the exceptional correction, then use uniqueness and pullback injectivity. -> effective exceptional trace correction
- **step-trace-positivity** [claim](#stmt-cor-big-trace) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-big-trace): Identify the positive current with the actual trace through vanishing of codimension-two currents, then produce a Kähler current. -> the trace class is big with local potentials
- **step-small-comparison** [claim](#stmt-lem-small-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-small-comparison): Subtract structure equations on a common resolution and apply negativity in both signs. -> effectivity or vanishing of the comparison divisor
- **step-integral-descent** [claim](#stmt-lem-descent) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-descent): Local base-point-freeness, constancy of the generated map on the fibre, and trivialization with consecutive multiples. -> actual line bundle descent with no extra power
- **step-supports** [claim](#stmt-lem-absolute-support) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-absolute-support): Compact slice of the cone, extreme point of the minimum face, finite cone theorem and strict separation. -> negative ray with supporting class and Kähler margin
- **step-relative-positivity** [claim](#stmt-lem-relative-positivity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-relative-positivity): Adjust a Hermitian metric by a global smooth function and use the fibrewise ampleness criterion. -> projectivity of the contraction
- **step-ordinary-step** [claim](#stmt-prop-ordinary-step) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-ordinary-step): Transcendental base-point-freeness with a Kähler margin, relative vanishing, descent for factoriality, finite generation and the analytic flip. -> contraction, divisorial step or flip with preserved category
- **step-class-transport** [claim](#stmt-lem-class-transport) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-class-transport): Unique decomposition over the base, comparison on a common resolution, negativity for the corrected trace. -> transport of the fixed trace across steps
- **step-cycle-loss** [claim](#stmt-lem-cycle-loss) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cycle-loss): Borel-Moore localization isomorphism, strict transforms of cycles and Kähler positivity of nonzero classes. -> surjection and strict drop of cycle spans
- **step-low-places** [claim](#stmt-lem-low-places) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-low-places): Coordinate discrepancy estimate on log smooth data, stratum blowups and counting of monomial places. -> finite low-discrepancy sets realized on a projective model
- **step-terminal-codim-two** [claim](#stmt-lem-terminal-codim-two) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-terminal-codim-two): Slice by a general surface and show a normal surface with positive discrepancies is smooth. -> smoothness in codimension two
- **step-low-centres** [claim](#stmt-lem-terminal-low-centres) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-terminal-low-centres): Log resolution with disjoint positive components and an iterated blowup count of places over fixed centres. -> surface centres of low places
- **step-terminal-flips** [claim](#stmt-prop-terminal-real-four) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-terminal-real-four): Induction over boundary coefficients using finite witness discrepancies, surface-class ranks and the cycle count. -> termination of terminal real flips
- **step-terminal-empty** [claim](#stmt-prop-terminal-four) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-terminal-four): Terminality persists, divisorial steps are finite by the cycle count, and a flip tail contradicts the flip theorem. -> termination for empty boundary
- **step-scaling-construction** [claim](#stmt-prop-scaling-construction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-scaling-construction): Define the nef threshold, find one ray occurring for infinitely many parameters, step, and transport the trace. -> scaling program with crepant threshold steps
- **step-thresholds-vanish** [claim](#stmt-lem-scaling-zero) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-scaling-zero): Marked weak log canonical models are finite over a compact parameter segment away from zero. -> thresholds tend to zero
- **step-extraction** [claim](#stmt-prop-terminal-limit) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-terminal-limit): Stabilize finite low-place sets, extract them on strongly factorial models, and pass to the coefficientwise limit. -> fixed terminal limiting pair
- **step-limiting-nef** [claim](#stmt-prop-limiting-nef) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-limiting-nef): Run the real-boundary program on the limiting pair and exclude divisorial and fibre steps by comparison with nef extracted adjoints. -> nef terminal model
- **step-rational-polytope** [claim](#stmt-lem-rational-nef-polytope) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-rational-nef-polytope): Bound curve intersections on extremal rays to get finitely many rational inequalities defining the nef locus. -> rational decomposition and positive gap
- **step-perturbation** [claim](#stmt-prop-trivial-perturbation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-trivial-perturbation): Amplified generalized adjoint, scaling program, gap argument forcing triviality on the limiting class, and descent of fixed indices. -> perturbed finite program trivial for the limit class
- **step-scaling-finite** [claim](#stmt-prop-scaling-finite) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-scaling-finite): Descend the limiting class to the original flip base on a common resolution and contradict negativity of the ray. -> finiteness of the scaling program
- **step-pe-preserved** [claim](#stmt-lem-pe-step) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-pe-step): Common resolution with an effective exceptional difference and descent in both directions. -> constant pseudo-effectivity status
- **step-mori-not-pe** [claim](#stmt-lem-mori-not-pe) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-mori-not-pe): Restrict a positive current to a fibre curve through a point of finite potential to get a nonnegative degree. -> Mori alternative excludes pseudo-effectivity
- **step-main-theorem** [claim](#stmt-thm-finite) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-mori-not-pe-2): Run the scaling program, which is finite, then read the endpoint from pseudo-effectivity and derive the discrepancy inequality from a common resolution. -> finite ordinary program with nef or Mori endpoint
- **step-local-counterexample** [claim](#stmt-prop-local-convention) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-local-convention): Explicit product construction with a nodal threefold resolution, curve classes and GAGA. -> necessity of the global convention

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-finite-ordinary-program**: Under the stated global factoriality, Kähler and klt hypotheses on a fourfold pair, one finite sequence of ordinary negative steps exists that starts on the given pair, and every model in it keeps the same category properties.
- **inv-nef-endpoint**: When the adjoint is pseudo-effective, the endpoint of the finite program has a nef adjoint and no prime divisor is extracted, with log discrepancies not decreasing.
- **inv-mori-not-pseudo-effective**: A rational line bundle whose negative is relatively ample over a strictly lower-dimensional base is not pseudo-effective, which separates the Mori fibre outcome from the nef outcome.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-local-factoriality**: Require every analytic local ring to be Q-factorial at every model of the program. -> A smooth projective fourfold exists whose only possible first negative contraction has a target that fails local analytic factoriality, so no program reaches a nef model under that stronger convention.
- **anti-hypotheses-scope**: Apply the finite-program conclusion to pairs outside the stated fourfold Kähler klt setting, or to real boundary data. -> The proof relies on the fourfold termination argument and on rational boundary data, so the conclusion is not guaranteed outside that regime.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The main result is an existence theorem in compact Kähler geometry: it asserts a finite sequence of negative steps built from cone, base-point-free and flip theorems on analytic spaces, with a proof by contradiction through limiting models. No finite procedure with computable inputs and outputs is given, so no runnable reference implementation or exact oracle exists.

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper proves an existence theorem about minimal model programs on compact Kähler fourfolds. It gives no executable procedure for choosing or performing contractions and flips, so there is nothing to implement as a library or program. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The objects are reduced irreducible complex analytic spaces with Kähler potentials. They have no discrete state, dynamics or interactive model that a simulation could reproduce. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Extremal rays are shown to exist by compactness and convex duality in a cone of currents. No computable cone or finite certificate is produced that a tool could output. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The scaling program has a stopping rule, but each step relies on non-constructive analytic theorems (cone, base-point-free and flip theorems), and finiteness is proved by contradiction. No effective algorithm with a complexity bound results. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The only counting invariant is a dimension of a span of analytic cycle classes in real homology, used as a termination measure. It is not a storable structure with operations. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Projectivity here means the existence of a relatively ample holomorphic line bundle. There is no numerical kernel or parallel computation to accelerate. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Factoriality of divisors on complex analytic spaces has no connection with secrecy, hardness assumptions or protocols. Nothing in the result bears on security. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The result concerns positivity of rational line bundles and the absence of positive currents. There is no data, loss function or learning procedure in it. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Finiteness of low-discrepancy places is a structural count, not an optimisation problem. The minimisation arguments inside proofs are used only to select rays and do not give a solvable program. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The program is a single sequential composition of birational steps with no concurrency, communication or fault model. The only systems-flavoured remark is that steps can be reapplied after a finite prefix. |
| other | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The natural home of the result is pure complex and birational geometry. It is not constructive enough for a verified executable artefact, and its limits (global rather than local factoriality, dimension four, rational boundary) are recorded elsewhere. |
