 \simeq\operatorname{pr}_1^*\mathcal O_{U_0}(1),$$ which is nef. On the blowup of the node the exceptional divisor has log discrepancy two; its smooth product with $C$ is a log resolution with the same log discrepancy. Hence the target is terminal, in particular klt.

It remains to check that the local divisor $P$ is not a global factoriality obstruction. The displayed integral cohomology of $U$ and Hodge decomposition give $H^1(U,\mathcal O_U)=H^2(U,\mathcal O_U)=0$ and $\mathop{\mathrm{Pic}}(U)=\mathbb Z[H|_U]\oplus\mathbb Z[E]$. The exponential sequence and Künneth therefore give $$\mathop{\mathrm{Pic}}(X)=\mathbb Z[H]\oplus\mathbb Z[D]\oplus\operatorname{pr}_C^*\mathop{\mathrm{Pic}}(C).$$ Strict transform and divisor pushforward identify $$\mathop{\mathrm{Cl}}(U_0\times C)=\mathop{\mathrm{Pic}}(X)/\mathbb Z[D].$$ Every surviving class descends to a Cartier class, either from the hyperplane bundle on $U_0$ or from a line bundle on $C$. Chow’s theorem makes every global analytic Weil divisor on this projective target algebraic, so every such divisor is Cartier (Serre 1956, sec. 4, no. 19, Proposition 13) <a id="pm-cite-58"></a>[[SerreGAGA1956](#ref-SerreGAGA1956)]. GAGA identifies its global coherent rank-one reflexive sheaves with algebraic divisorial sheaves, which are therefore invertible as well (Serre 1956, sec. 3, no. 12, Theorems 2–3) <a id="pm-cite-59"></a>[[SerreGAGA1956](#ref-SerreGAGA1956)]. The plane $P$ is defined only on an analytic neighbourhood; it need not extend to a divisor on the whole compact space. ◻

</details>

  "figures": [
    {
      "caption": "The finite-program argument. The ordinary sequence starts on $X$. Extraction and the limiting and nearby programs are auxiliary comparisons. Exact bundle descent preserves the indices used in the positive intersection gap; the last descent occurs on a common resolution, as in Figure 2.",
      "figure_id": "fig:dependencies",
      "pdf_page": 4,
      "source_evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "fig:dependencies",
          "exact_quote": "The finite-program argument. The ordinary sequence starts on\n$X$. Extraction and the limiting and nearby programs are auxiliary\ncomparisons. Exact bundle descent preserves the indices used in the\npositive intersection gap; the last descent ",
          "source_object": "fig:0001"
        }
      ],
      "source_kind": "tikz_pgf",
      "tikz_pgf_source": "\\begin{tikzpicture}[\n box/.style={draw=linkblue!65,rounded corners=2pt,align=center,\n fill=linkblue!3,text width=5.1cm,inner sep=6pt,font=\\small},\n edge/.style={-{Stealth[length=2mm]},draw=linkblue!80,thick}]\n\\node[box] (steps) at (0,0)\n {Positive traces and ordinary steps\\\\Exact line-bundle descent};\n\\node[box] (terminal) at (6.2,0)\n {Discrepancies and analytic cycle ranks\\\\Terminal fourfold termination\\\\with real boundary};\n\\node[box] (scaling) at (0,-2)\n {Ordinary scaling from $X$\\\\Vanishing thresholds\\\\Stabilized auxiliary extraction};\n\\node[box] (limit) at (6.2,-2)\n {The limiting terminal pair\\\\A finite small program\\\\to a nef ordinary adjoint};\n\\node[box] (gap) at (0,-4.1)\n {Rational nef decomposition\\\\Fixed Cartier indices\\\\and a positive intersection gap};\n\\node[box] (perturb) at (6.2,-4.1)\n {A nearby generalized program\\\\Crepant rational adjoints\\\\Descent to the original flip base};\n\\node[box,text width=11.3cm] (finite) at (3.1,-6.1)\n {The original negative ray becomes trivial: contradiction\\\\\n A finite ordinary program and its nef or Mori endpoint\n (Theorem~\\ref{thm:finite})};\n\\draw[edge] (steps)--(scaling);\n\\draw[edge] (terminal)--(limit);\n\\draw[edge] (scaling)--(limit);\n\\draw[edge] (limit.south west)--(gap.north east);\n\\draw[edge] (gap)--(perturb);\n\\draw[edge] (limit)--(perturb);\n\\draw[edge] (perturb)--(finite);\n\\end{tikzpicture}",
      "witness_description": "A dependency flowchart arranged in four horizontal tiers with downward arrows between rounded rectangular boxes:\n- Tier 1: Two boxes side by side. Left box: \"Positive traces / and ordinary steps / Exact line-bundle descent\". Right box: \"Discrepancies and / analytic cycle ranks / Terminal fourfold termination / with real boundary\".\n- Tier 2: Two boxes side by side. Left box: \"Ordinary scaling from X / Vanishing thresholds / Stabilized auxiliary extraction\". Right box: \"The limiting terminal pair / A finite small program / to a nef ordinary adjoint\". Downward vertical arrow from Tier 1 left box to Tier 2 left box; downward vertical arrow from Tier 1 right box to Tier 2 right box.\n- Tier 3: Two boxes side by side. Left box: \"Rational nef decomposition / Fixed Cartier indices / and a positive intersection gap\". Right box: \"A nearby generalized program / Crepant rational adjoints / Descent to the original flip base\". Downward vertical arrow from Tier 2 left box to Tier 3 left box; downward diagonal arrow from Tier 2 left box to Tier 3 right box; downward vertical arrow from Tier 2 right box to Tier 3 right box.\n- Tier 4: A single wide box: \"The original negative ray becomes trivial: contradiction / A finite ordinary program and its nef or Mori endpoint (Theorem 1.2)\". Two inward diagonal downward arrows lead to this box, one from Tier 3 left box and one from Tier 3 right box."
    },
    {
      "caption": "The final comparison is made on $V$. The class $p^{*}P_{Wi}$ descends along $r$; no morphism from $W_{i}$ to $X_{i}$ or to $Z_{i}$ is required.",
      "figure_id": "fig:perturbation-descent",
      "pdf_page": 30,
      "source_evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "fig:perturbation-descent",
          "exact_quote": "The final comparison is made on $V$.\n The class $p^*P_{W_i}$ descends along $r$; no morphism from $W_i$\n to $X_i$ or to $Z_i$ is required.",
          "source_object": "fig:0002"
        }
      ],
      "source_kind": "tikz_pgf",
      "tikz_pgf_source": "\\begin{tikzpicture}[\n   >=Latex,\n   every node/.style={font=\\small},\n   map/.style={->,semithick}\n ]\n  \\node (V) at (0,2.5) {$V$};\n  \\node (Wi) at (-2.4,1.25) {$W_i$};\n  \\node (Yi) at (2.4,1.25) {$Y_i$};\n  \\node (Xi) at (2.4,0) {$X_i$};\n  \\node (Zi) at (0,-1.15) {$Z_i$};\n  \\draw[map] (V)--node[above left] {$p$}(Wi);\n  \\draw[map] (V)--node[above right] {$q$}(Yi);\n  \\draw[map] (Yi)--node[right] {$\\mu_i$}(Xi);\n  \\draw[map] (Xi)--node[below right] {$f_i$}(Zi);\n  \\draw[map] (V)--node[left,pos=.63] {$r=f_i\\mu_iq$}(Zi);\n  \\draw[dashed,semithick] (Wi)--node[above,pos=.28] {small}(Yi);\n \\end{tikzpicture}",
      "witness_description": "A commutative diagram showing maps between five geometric spaces:\n- Top: node labeled V.\n- Middle row: node labeled W_i on the left, node labeled Y_i on the right.\n- Below Y_i: node labeled X_i.\n- Bottom: node labeled Z_i.\n- Directed arrows and labels:\n  - Arrow pointing down-left from V to W_i labeled p.\n  - Arrow pointing down-right from V to Y_i labeled q.\n  - Horizontal dashed double-headed arrow connecting W_i and Y_i labeled \"small\" above the dashed line.\n  - Vertical arrow pointing downwards from Y_i to X_i labeled \\mu_i.\n  - Arrow pointing down-left from X_i to Z_i labeled f_i.\n  - Long curved arrow from V curving down and left to Z_i labeled r = f_i\\mu_i q."
    }
  ],
